## Document 053: 2308台達電 群益

Source PDF: all report pdf/2308台達電 群益.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-06-04
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 6 月 4 日

1 群益投顧 
台達電 (2308 TT) 於 COMPUTEX 2026 以「 Superior Efficiency, Shaping Sustainable 
AI 」 為 主 題 ， 提 出 多 項 創 新 解 決 方 案。 燃 料 電 池 與 固 態 變 壓 器 為 長 期 成 長 動 能 ，預期
2~3 年內將有較顯著營收貢獻 。台 達 電 為 AI 伺服器電源解決方案與水冷 Sidecar 主要
供 應 商，受 惠 於 AI、Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長，營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢，維 持Buy
投 資 評 等 ， 目 標 價 為 2,800 元 。

研 究 員 ： 王 美 珍   
iris.wang@capital.com.tw

燃 料 電 池 與 固 態 變 壓 器為 長 期 成 長 動 能：  
燃 料 電 池 具 備 高 效 率 、 可 堆 疊 特 性 。 公 司 已 規 劃 於 觀 音 廠 於2026 年 底 前 完
成 第 一 條 生 產 線 安 裝 ， 並 已 在 籌 備 觀 音 二 廠 擴 大 規 模 。 
在未來的 2~3 年 內 ， 包 含 燃 料 電 池 預 計 僅 佔 總 營 收約 1% ， 但 其 作 為 長 期 能
源 解 決 方 案 的 成 長 曲 線 非 常 明 確 。 
SST 目 前 已 少 量 出 貨 ， 客 戶 為 中 系 CSP 、 800V 高 壓 直 流 電 動 車 充 電 站 ， 亦
出 貨 給 美 系 客 戶 測 試 。SST 導 入 需 從 資 料 中 心 設 計 初 期 即 納 入 規 劃 ， 預 期 
2~3 年 後 會 產 生 顯 著 的 營 收 貢 獻 ， 仍 持 續 改 良 產 品 規 格 中 。 
雖然 SST 可 能 影 響 傳 統 AC/DC 電 源 業 務，但 SST 單 價 遠 高 於 電 源產品 ，應
是有利 。 台 達 電 在 AC/DC 電 源 業 務 市 佔 率極 高， 目前 亦 為 SST 進 度 最 快 的
廠商 ， 預 期 台 達 電 在 SST 仍 可 維 持 高市 佔 率 。  
台 達 電 為 AI 伺 服 器 電 源 解 決 方 案 與 水 冷 Sidecar 主 要 供 應 商 ：  
為 AI 伺 服 器 電 源 解 決 方 案 主 要 供 應 商 。 受 惠 於 AI 需 求 提 升 ， 帶 動 AC/DC
電 源 大 幅 成 長 外 ，CSP 客 戶 在 新 一 代 ASIC 伺 服 器 平 台 採 用 DC/DC 電 源 模
組，亦 將 成 為 另 一 成 長 動 能。 NVIDIA 主 導 的 800 VDC 進 度 提 早 一 年，預 計
2026 年 生 產 並 出 貨 ；± 400VDC  2026 年 亦 會出貨。  
水冷散熱 以 Sidecar 為主 ， 2025 年、 1Q26 營收比重為 9% 。 台 達 電 Sidecar
產品 2025 年 市 佔 率 極 高， 雖 有 競 爭 者 加 入， 但 整 體 市 場 需 求 持 續 增 長 ， 故
2026 年 Sidecar 營 收 仍 將 維 持 成 長 趨 勢 。 
投 資 建 議 ：  
台達電 為 AI 伺 服 器 電 源 解 決 方 案與 水冷 Sidecar 主 要 供 應 商， 受惠於 AI 、
Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長，營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢，維持 Buy 投 資 評 等，
目標價為 2,800 元 。

相 關 個 股 獲 利 預 估 、 評 價 及 投 資 建 議

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 6 月 4 日

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

2027, with a 40% Y/Y volume growth target. 2nm tape-out appears on track for late
2026, with production and shipments expected for 4Q27.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Alchip Technologies Ltd (3661.TW)
Base case value, derived from a residual income model. We assume the cost of equity is con-
stant at 10.4% (2% risk-free rate, 6% risk premium, 1.4 beta). Our intermediate growth rate and
terminal growth rates are 16% and 5%, respectively.
Risks to Upside
n Demand for Chinese GPUs and AI chipsets takes off earlier than expected.
n NRE demand accelerates.

n Alchip wins more US hyperscaler HPC projects.
Risks to Downside
n China's HPC localization progress slows down.
n AI chipset progress is much slower to develop.
n NRE demand decelerates.
n Pricing competition intensifies.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 067: 260601_daiwa_Delta

Source PDF: all report pdf/260601_daiwa_Delta.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-06-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide an update on Delta after our 2026 Taiwan 
Corporate Day. We reaffirm our positive view on Delta and view it as one of 
our top ideas within the AI trend.

What’s the impact: Position in the power segment remains resilient. 
Based on our understanding, we believe there are four potential power 
architecture solutions to be applied in the Vera Rubin generation, with two 
from a high-voltage environment and the other two from a low-voltage 
environment. The high-voltage environment could be divided into 800VDC 
or +-400VDC, while the total voltage from rack could range from 600kW to 
1.5MW, depending on client requirements. As for low-voltage options, these 
can either be 72kW powershelf (bundled with 6x of 12kW PSUs) or 110kW 
powershelf (bundled with 6x of Nvidia’s standard 18.3kW solution), but 
width of 18.3kW power supply will be 3x bigger than Delta’s 12kW solution. 
We assume Nvidia’s strategy to use HVDC has sped up by c.1 year, and it 
would have 20-30% of HVDC adoption from this generation and 50% from 
2027. This is the key driver of our higher AI server revenue assumption.  
New growth drivers anticipated by investors. During Taiwan Corporate 
Day, we believe investor interest has gradually shifted to fuel cells (ie, 
SOFC or SOEC). Due to the technology licenses from Ceres Power (CWR 
L), Delta plans to build up trial fuel cell-capacity from 4Q26 and start mass 
production in 2027. We believe investors are keen to know the competition 
between Ceres and Bloom Energy in terms of tech spec and Delta’s 
competitors (Weichai [2338 HK, HKD40.14, Outperform (2); covered by 
Kelvin Lau] and Doosan Enerbility [034020 KS, KRW106,900, Buy (1); 
covered by Mike Oh]), which are also licensed from Ceres Power. We 
believe it will become the next growth driver for Delta after HVDC plays out. 
The micro grid (vs. utility grid) concept with rising new-type energy 
generation method (eg, fuel cells), solid-state transformers (SSTs), Energy 
Storage System (ESS) could enable Delta to further transform itself from a 
power systems company into an infrastructure provider.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 8-9% to reflect 
better-than-expected gross margin performance and AI server contributions 
on rapid adoption of HVDC from Nvidia. We raise our 12M TP to TWD2,922 
(from TWD2,440), based on a higher target PER of 55x (from 50x; above 
its past-3-year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS, due to 
new growth drivers from the micro grid. We believe a higher target PER is 
justified as the PEG ratio stands at 0.90x (vs. our 2025-28E earnings 
CAGR of 61%). Downside risk: weaker-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 4-36% above Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more bullish HVDC adoption rate.

1 June 2026

2026 Taiwan corporate day conference note

➢ Positive AI server power development with faster HVDC adoption  
➢ New growth drivers from fuel cell, SST, ESS and micro grid  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lifting 12-month TP to TWD2,922

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

Japan - Notification items pursuant to Article 37 of the Financial Instruments and Exchange Law 
(This Notification is only applicable to where report is distributed by Daiwa Securities Co. Ltd.)    
If you decide to enter into a business arrangement with our company based on the information described in this report, we ask you to pay close attention to the following items.  
• In addition to the purchase price of a financial instrument, our company will collect a trading commission* for each transaction as agreed beforehand with you. Since commissions may be 
included in the purchase price or may not be charged for certain transactions, we recommend that you confirm the commission for each transaction. In some cases, our company also may 
charge a maximum of ¥2 million per year as a standing proxy fee for our deposit of your securities, if you are a non-resident.  
• For derivative and margin transactions etc., our company may require collateral or margin requirements in accordance with an agreement made beforehand with you. Ordinarily in such cases, 
the amount of the transaction will be in excess of the required collateral or margin requirements**.  
• There is a risk that you will incur losses on your transactions due to changes in the market price of financial instruments based on fluctuations in interest rates, exchange rates, stock prices, real 
estate prices, commodity prices, and others. In addition, depending on the content of the transaction, the loss could exceed the amount of the collateral or margin requirements.  
• There may be a difference between bid price etc. and ask price etc. of OTC derivatives handled by our company.  
• Before engaging in any trading, please thoroughly confirm accounting and tax treatments regarding your trading in financial instruments with such experts as certified public accountants.  
*  The amount of the trading commission cannot be stated here in advance because it will be determined between our company and you based on current market conditions and the content of 
each transaction etc. 
**  The ratio of margin requirements etc. to the amount of the transaction cannot be stated here in advance because it will be determined between our company and you based on current market 
conditions and the content of each transaction etc.

the signing of the agreement with our company.

M
Update
WinWay Technology Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific
Asia   AI Summit 2026 Feedback
Larger package size drives testing interface demand: As testing time continues to
increase, the pin count per socket has increased from 4-6k to over 10k, and
management expects this trend to  continue in the next few years as chips become
more and more complex. Incremental testing steps, such as functional burn-in or
SL T, also expand the TAM for Winway.
Burn-in socket:  This was not the key focus for Winway in the past, but it said it will

now try
to target the high-end segment instead of dealing with pricing competition
in the low-end market once it expands its  new capacity in the next few years.
Capacity expansion plan: Winway expects its pogo pin capacity for test sockets to
reach 9mn/month by end-2026, then 14mn/month by 1H27, mainly from its Renwu
Facility 1. Renwu Facility 2 is expected to start in 2Q27, further lifting capacity to
14mn/month. The current pogo-pin self-sufficiency ratio is ~35-45%; management

Valuation Methodology and Risks
WinWay Technology Co Ltd (6515.TW)
We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture
long-term value. We assume a 9.14% cost of equity (risk-free rate 2.0% and risk premium
5.5%), a payout ratio of 80%, a medium-term growth rate of 16.4%, and a terminal growth rate
of 4.5%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected adoption of hypersockets and MEMS probe cards

n Faster-than-expected SL T and burn-in socket introduction
n Stronger-than-expected edge AI demand
Risks to Downside
n Slower-than-expected adoption of hypersockets and MEMS probe cards
n Slower-than-expected SL T and burn-in socket introduction
n No edge AI demand in the next few years

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 081: 260601_ms_yageo

Source PDF: all report pdf/260601_ms_yageo.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-06-01
Pages: 25
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
 Idea
Why has the stock rallied so much over the past month? There is fear that leading 
Japanese and Korean MLCC suppliers are shifting more capacity to cater to the high 
end,  resulting in undersupply for the midrange/low end. The same narrative that 
drove the 2018 cycle is now back in play. 
So what is happening?

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
§ = Consensus data is provided by Refinitiv Estimates
e = Morgan Stanley Research estimates

### Page 3

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 3
Investment Thesis
What does the supply/demand balance look like? Mapping out supply and demand has
always been very difficult for the MLCC market. Companies typically do not disclose their
capacity data; even for companies that do, it is often disclosed in amount of units per
month, or per year. However, there are different case sizes and layer counts for MLCC,
which all need to be taken into account when trying to calculate  supply. Similarly, this

becomes a challenge when modeling demand as well. Thus, it is hard to build a bottom-up
supply/demand model. That said...
Here is what we are hearing from the supply chain
• Utilization rates are increasing... Yageo attended our Asia AI Summit last week,
and mentioned that 2Q utilization  for all standard and premium products is
running  higher than its guidance during the 1Q earnings call; currently standard
products are running at 75%+ and premium at 85%+. According to Yahoo news

today, Chairman Pierre Chen even mentioned at Yageo's recent AGM that
utilization rates will reach 80%+ for standard products and 90%+ for premium,
though he did not mention a specific time for when this will happen.
• ...so suppliers are expecting pricing increase for the midrange/low end: Lead-
times are also extending, and this gives suppliers better bargaining power with end
customers to raise prices for the midrange/low end, where prices and profitability

are not as good. As a result, we expect to see price increases for midrange/low-end
MLCCs, though not yet to the extent of 2018, when distributor pricing increased
~10x. For the high end, it appears that most suppliers are still waiting to see if
Murata raises prices and will only follow Murata. However, according to Shoji Sato,
Murata doesn't plan to raise prices for existing MLCC products.
• Distributors and ODMs are both building inventory: Our checks indicate that

both distributors and ODMs are asking for more MLCC and buying as much as they
can to build inventory. The distributors are scared of supply shortage, while the
ODMs are trying to make sure that MLCC does not become the next bottleneck in
the supply chain.
AI demand is growing
We estimate MLCC dollar content to rise ~182% for VR200 NVL72 vs. GB300 NVL72:
Based on our latest estimates, we estimate MLCC content for VR200 to be ~US$4.3K.
This would present a quite meaningful increase vs. GB300 at only ~US$1.5K, and could

explain why high-end AI server MLCC demand is currently so strong and causing all the
ODMs to aggressively trying to secure and build as much inventory as possible, ahead of
the Rubin rack ramp from 2H26 onwards. Our checks indicate that the MLCC content per
computing board and switch board is increasing quite meaningfully, with the computing
board MLCC content showing a larger content increase. In addition, the newly introduced
BlueField and ConnectX modules will also contribute to more MLCC content per rack.

Total MLCC unit demand is rising ~80% for a VR200 NVL72 rack vs a GB300 NVL72
rack: Based on our estimate, we think there is ~570K units of MLCCs within an entire

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Rubin NVL72 rack, which is meaningfully higher than a GB300 NVL72 rack at ~320k units
of MLCCs ( Exhibit 2 ). This increase comes from increasing content per board for the
computing board and switch board, but also increased PCB content, such as the CX9
Orchid Module. This increase in unit count is one of the main drivers behind the 182%
increase in dollar content.
This  means that a higher proportion of high-cap MLCCs is driving the other ~100%

growth: We also see increasing demand for high-cap MLCCs (47uF+), where we see these
high-cap MLCCs rising to 30%+ of the mix in entire Rubin system. This would mean a
meaningful increase in demand vs. GB300 (<20%) –   Exhibit 3 . Under our assumption,
47uF+ MLCC demand would rise quite dramatically, from a unit perspective. Our estimate
would imply that 47uF+ MLCC demand would rise 210% for a VR200 rack vs. GB300,
while 47uF and below MLCC content would rise at a lower magnitude of ~50%.

Stronger demand may cause a crowding out of capacity for suppliers that are geared
more towards the high end: This meaningful increase in MLCC volume per rack,
especially that of high-cap MLCCs. is exactly what distributors and ODMs  fear: that this
would cause a crowding out of capacity for the midrange/low-end. With that said, Yageo is
relatively less levered to the high-cap/high-end MLCC market for AI servers, vs. its
Japanese or Korean peers. However, Yageo still has good exposure in AI servers for MLCCs

that are 22uF or below. In addition, Yageo also plays a crucial role in supplying tantalum
capacitors into AI servers – it is currently the largest tantalum capacitor supplier globally.
Tantalum revenue was ~22% in CY25 but we see that rising to ~25%+ in CY26-28. And in
terms of profits, tantalum capacitors also carry very high margins, by our estimate (45%+
at least), and so we expect profit to rise from <30% of the mix in CY25 to 30% or higher
in CY26-28.

For more analysis of our Rubin Rack BOM analysis, please see here: Greater China
Technology Hardware: Analysis of Rubin rack BOM, component content, and ODM value-
added (20 May 2026)

Tantalum capacitor pricing may show further price increases
in 2H
Exhibit 4 shows that the prices of tantalum pentoxide have been increasing since early

2025, and have risen even more sharply since early 2026. In response, Yageo has been
taking corresponding pricing actions to reflect the inflationary raw material costs as well –
it has announced pricing adjustments for its tantalum products at least three times since
April 2025, based on our supply chain checks. With tantalum pentoxide pricing having
risen 100%+ since late 2025, we believe Yageo is highly likely to implement another round
of price adjustments for its tantalum products in the near term, which should further

improve its profitability.

Raising estimates for Yageo
2Q tracking ahead of guidance: As mentioned above, utilization rate is tracking above its
previous guidance given during its 1Q earnings call. Hence, we raise our 2Q revenue
forecast 5%, to NT$41.9bn (+10% q/q) - 2% above the Street, with GM of 39.0% (+90bps
q/q) and OpM of 26.8% (+160bps q/q) for EPS of NT$4.21 –  1% above the Street.
We update our model to reflect the potential for, continued price hikes, primarily for

tantalum capacitors and MLCCs, driven by AI demand. Our 2026-28 EPS estimates
increase 7%, 23%, and 53%, respectively, driven by higher assumptions for both revenues
and margins.

### Page 10

M
 Idea
10
Key catalyst
Monthly revenue shows signs of acceleration: This is always something the market 
tends to focus on when trading MLCC stocks, and we are starting to see signs of 
acceleration at Yageo. With demand continuing to see improvement and if pricing 
adjustments happens in 2H, we expect to see further acceleration of monthly revenue, and 
this would be positive for the stock. We have observed this consistent relationship over 
the past decade, and we don’t think this time will be any different. 
Exhibit 7: Yageo's monthly sales y/y are starting to show signs of acceleration
Source: TEJ, Morgan Stanley Research.

Where there could be risks 
The obvious risk is a sudden bursting of demand for AI. However, the likelihood of this 
happening is relatively low, considering what we know today around increasing GPU rental 
prices, real use cases for AI, etc. Nonetheless, this would be the most obvious risk, but it 
also would affect almost all the companies in the supply chain, and not just MLCC players. 
That said, other areas we are monitoring are inventory risk and consumer electronics 
demand risk, particularly in 2H26-1H27. At our Asia AI Summit, Yageo mentioned that 
distributor inventory remains healthy at <5 months (vs. slightly above 5 months in 1Q). 
However. since inventory level is based on the current end demand, a meaningfully lower 
end demand environment would imply that the same amount of MLCC inventory for 
distributors would take much longer to clear out. In that scenario, pricing pressure would 
likely follow shortly after.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
We raise our 12-month price target to NT$1,010 (from NT$360): This reflects our
higher earnings estimates for CY26-28 and our raised medium-term growth rate
assumption. Our price target is our base case value, derived from our residual income
valuation model. Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our
coverage, we think this methodology provides the most accurate value of the firm by

taking into account the cost of equity.
Our key assumptions are:
• Cost of equity of 7.8%:
° Risk-free rate of 1.0% (10-year Taiwanese government bond yield);
° Equity risk premium of 6.8%;
° Beta of 1.1;
• Medium-term growth rate of 16% (raised from 10%);
• Terminal growth rate of 3%.
Our price target implies 42.8x 2027e P/E and 31.7x 2028e P/E. These are above its five-
year historical range of 8x to 18x, which we view as justified considering its longer-term

transformation story to become a global total solutions provider, moving from passive
into active components. In addition, compared to its global passive component peers, we
believe Yageo stock remains undervalued with higher ROE and margins, but lower P/E vs.
Japanese peers (which trade on an average at 25x+ P/E).
Our bull and bear case scenario values increase by magnitudes similar to the change to
our base case value, to NT$1,530 and NT$505, respectively.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

the next 12-18 months.

12-18 months.

## Document 083: 260602_gs_auras

### Page 1

Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C.
Auras (3324.TWO): Computex 2026: Liquid cooling total solution with
in-row CDU showcased; Buy
2 June 2026 | 11:26PM HKT
We visited Auras’ booth on June 2 at our Computex tour in Taipei. Auras
showcased its liquid cooling and air cooling solution, covering components across
CDU (Cooling Distribution Unit), UQD (Universal Quick Disconnect), cold plate,
manifold etc. Management highlighted the rising adoption of liquid cooling solution,

driven by the AI infrastructure requiring higher efficienc y and lower power
consumption. The company has launched in-row CDU to work under 3 degree ATC
(Approach Temperature Differ ence) environment, and a water pump product to
support PWM (Pulse Width Modulation) demand. We expect to see triple digit YoY
earnings growth in 2026E, with rising contribution from liquid cooling and higher
blended GM. Maintain Buy.
Key takeaways
1. Rising liquid cooling penetration: Overall, management is positive on the rising

adoption of liquid cooling solutions to lower the power consumption of AI
infrastructure and the total operational cost. The company has expanded from GPU
/ CPU cooling solution to ASIC, and the company’s CPU / DIMM (Dual In-line Memory
Module) dual cooling module is featured with high accuracy and reliability,
supporting rising CPU workload and AI data management.
2. Cooling products in expansion: The company showcased its liquid cooling total
solutions for AI servers across various architectures. Management highlighted the

in-row CSU could support up to 2MW cooling efficiency under 3 degree ATC
(Approach Temperature Difference) environment. Besides offering UQD and MQD
(Multiple Quick Disconnect), mgmt noted Auras also develops the water pump
products for PWM (Pulse Width Modulation) to balance the performance and power
consumption, which is used across AI data center, work station and Notebook.
Goldman Sachs does and seeks to do business with companies covered in its research reports. As a result, investors

www.gs.com/research/hedge.html
. Analysts employed by non-US affiliates are not registered/qualified as research
analysts with FINRA in the U.S.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Auras
Our 12m target price for Auras is based on a near-term P/E, consistent with our Taiwan
Technology coverage. We base our 12-m TP of NT$1,283 on a target P/E multiple of
17.9x forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between
P/E and EPS growth of its peers in technology hardware, based on the company’s
2027E-28E EPS YoY growth at 23%. Key downside risks: 1) Slower-than-expected liquid

cooling penetration; (2) Soft AI server demand; (3) Weaker-than-expected PC market;
and (4) Potential competition from existing and new suppliers.

GS Factor Profile
The Goldman Sachs Factor Profile provides investment context for a stock by comparing key attributes to the market (i.e. our universe of rated stocks)
and its sector peers. The four key attributes depicted are: Growth, Financial Returns, Multiple (e.g. valuation) and Integrated (a c omposite of Growth,
Financial Returns and Multiple). Growth, Financial Returns and Multiple are calculated by using normalized ranks for specific metrics f or each stock. The

normalized ranks for the metrics are then averaged and converted into percentiles for the relevant attribute. The precise calcula tion of each metric may
vary depending on the fiscal y ear, industry and region, but the standard approach is as follows:
Growth is based on a stock’s forward-looking sales growth, EBITDA growth and EPS growth (for financial s tocks, only EPS and sales growth), with a
higher percentile indicating a higher growth company. Financial Returns is based on a stock’s forward-looking ROE, ROCE and CROCI (for financial

stocks, only ROE), with a higher percentile indicating a company with higher financial r eturns. Multiple is based on a stock’s forward-looking P/E, P/B,
price/dividend (P/D), EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF and EV/Debt Adjusted Cash Flow (DACF) (for financial s tocks, only P/E, P/B and P/D), with a higher percentile
indicating a stock trading at a higher multiple. The Integrated percentile is calculated as the average of the Growth percentile, Financial Returns

percentile and (100% - Multiple percentile).
Financial Returns and Multiple use the Goldman Sachs analyst forecasts at the fiscal y ear-end at least three quarters in the future. Growth uses inputs
for the fiscal y ear at least seven quarters in the future compared with the year at least three quarters in the future (on a per-share basis for all metrics).
For a more detailed description of how we calculate the GS Factor Profile, please c ontact your GS representative.

M&A Rank
Across our global coverage, we examine stocks using an M&A framework, considering both qualitative factors and quantitative factors (which may vary
across sectors and regions) to incorporate the potential that certain companies could be acquired. We then assign a M&A rank as a means of sc oring
companies under our rated coverage from 1 to 3, with 1 representing high (30%-50%) probability of the company becoming an acquisition targ et, 2
representing medium (15%-30%) probability and 3 representing low (0%-15%) probability. For companies ranked 1 or 2, in line with our s tandard

departmental guidelines we incorporate an M&A component into our target price. M&A rank of 3 is considered immaterial and theref ore does not
factor into our price target, and may or may not be discussed in research.
Quantum
Quantum is Goldman Sachs’ proprietary database providing access to detailed financial statement histories, forecasts and ratios. It can be used for
in-depth analysis of a single company, or to make comparisons between companies in differ ent sectors and markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a B uy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not C overed), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region ’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the r eturn across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment R eview Committee or

other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such s tocks.
Total return potential represents the upside or downside differ ential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all cov ered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership .

Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at
https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Update
AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
May Sales -10% MoM/+40%
YoY
Here we focus on AirTAC's monthly sales, which we believe
could be a catalyst for its recent share price movement.
What's new:
• May sales came in at NT$3,909mn (-10% MoM/+40% YoY), with the
sequential decline mainly due to the fewer working days amid the May
holidays in China.
• By segment, shipments from battery, machine tool, general machinery and
textiles  outperformed during the month.

• QTD revenue reached 73% of MSe at NT$11,342mn (+13% QoQ/+27% YoY)
and 75% of consensus at NT$10,941mn (+9% QoQ/+22% YoY).
Our view:
• We see 2Q26 revenue tracking ahead of expectations.
•  Orders for the month continued to exceed shipments in April, and both are
above its own expectations.
• Management indicated that its operating margin has reached a record high in
April and could continue to expand. We do not have its monthly history, but
its record-high single-quarter operating margin was 35.3% in 2Q10, so It

implies it has surpassed the above level in April on a single month basis (vs.
2Q26 consensus at 34.5%).
• It believes that the impact from rising material costs should be manageable
owing to its raised selling prices in the overseas market and efficiency/
product mix improvement.
• We stay OW on AirTAC amid the industry upcycle and its consistent share
gains. Moreover, current valuation of 23x 2027e P/E seems undemanding vs.
its mid-cycle average of 25x, in our view.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 30x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along with
business cycles). As the industry enters an upcycle, we think applying a peak cycle valuation
of 30x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

800V HVDC: From Server Power Upgrade to Full AI Factory Architecture Shift  —
On the power system, the shift to 800V HVDC is not just a PSU upgrade, but a full
AI rack architecture change. Traditional AC systems require multiple conversion
stages from UPS, distribution, server PSU and low-voltage DC delivery, causing
power loss, heat, copper weight and cabling complexity. 800V HVDC reduces

conversion steps and brings high-voltage DC closer to compute racks, supporting
future rack density from around 100kW toward 500kW–1MW. This changes the
supplier’s opportunity. Busbar, cable and connector vendors benefit from higher
demand for compact high-voltage DC busbars, 125A+ connectors, hot-swap
design, insulation, arcing protection and rack-level safety. PSU suppliers move
from server PSU modules toward centralized power shelves and power racks

integrating rectifiers, DC/DC conversion, BBU, monitoring and protection. Cooling
also becomes more critical: higher rack power makes liquid cooling mandatory,
driving CDU, cold plates, manifolds, leak detection and quick-disconnect demand.
Overall, 800V HVDC turns AI infrastructure from server assembly into power-
cooling-compute system integration, favoring suppliers with system design,
reliability and safety certification capability. We are positive on Hon Hai, Delta and

AVC.
ASPEED: From BMC to Server-Control Platform —  Global No. 1 BMC maker
ASPEED also demonstrates its product roadmap and is moving from a traditional
BMC upgrade cycle toward a broader server-control platform for AI servers. The
core BMC transition is AST2700/2750, which starts ramping in 2H26, followed by
AST2800, targeted for mass production in 2028. AST2800 carries roughly 2x the
cost of AST2700, implying higher ASP , specifications, more advanced process

adoption and stronger content per server. Beyond BMC, ASPEED is expanding into
SMC, PRo T, I/O expander and eFPGA SoC products. AST1040, the next-generation
SMC, is expected to be production-ready in 1H27, while AST1840, the eFPGA SoC,
should ramp in 2H27. The company also plans to integrate Caliptra into new
generations of all chips, highlighting the rising importance of server security,
platform root-of-trust and remote-management functions. The bigger trend is

that AI servers require more telemetry, security, control and firmware-level
coordination, making BMC/SMC less like low-value support chips and more like
essential infrastructure controllers.  T ogether with various racks including existing
NVL72 and ASIC business, the new CPU, LPU, storage and SPX racks from Nvidia,
we expect strong BMC growth in the longer term to persist.

Asia Vital Components
(3017.TW; NT$2700.0; 1; 02 Jun 26; 13:30)
Valuation
We value AVC using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum
and the expansion of its addressable market. Our TP of NT$3,450 is derived
from applying a 25x PE multiple on 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified
given the company’s entrenched positioned in the server thermal segment,

rising contribution from liquid cooling, and the increasing value of thermal
content per server as power density continues to climb. With these structural
drivers still in place, we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its
historical averages.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported by other qualitative factors such as cold-
plate leadership and deep CSP penetration, and so a High Risk rating has not

been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target price
include: 1) slower adoption pace for liquid cooling adoption; 2) heightened
price competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling
components; and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.
Delta Electronics
(2308.TW; NT$2360.0; 1; 02 Jun 26; 13:30)
Valuation
Our target price for Delta Electronics of NT$2,480 is based on 40x 2027E EPS
estimate. Our target multiple is set at the higher end of its trading range since

2023, which we believe is warranted by Delta’s strong earnings growth and
better product mix prospects. We also believe the company’s leading industry
position, long product development cycle, and strong engagement with
customers should translate into high orders/earnings visibility.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
are: 1) lower contribution from non-PC businesses; 2) products in the new
businesses (LED lighting, electric vehicle, and Voice) growing more slowly

than expected; and 3) PC power supply losing market share.
Hon Hai Precision
(2317.TW; NT$301.5; 1; 02 Jun 26; 13:30)
Valuation
Our target price of NT$360 is based on 20x 2026E EPS, which is at the higher
end of the historical trading range. We believe this multiple is justified by the
company’s enhanced sales growth and profit margin outlook, as well as new

03 June 2026 Citi Research
6
endeavors into AI server, EV, digital health and robotics. Hon Hai has been
able to generate positive earnings streams, and thus we believe a P/E
valuation methodology is appropriate.
Risks
Downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) slower-than-expected iPhone demand; 2) intensified peer
competition; 3) weaker-than-expected execution on new business progress;

and 4) higher-than-expected impact of COVID-19 or other macro factors.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C., Taipei

Elite Material (2383.TW): Taiwan Computex & Corporate Day: Capacity
expansion to capture doubled demand from major ASIC project in 2027
3 June 2026 | 10:50AM CST
We hosted meetings with EMC at our Taiwan Computex & Corporate Day (June
2nd). Key takeaways include:
(1) For a specific CSP cus tomer, EMC is expected to gain market share in 2027 as
demand will double while Japanese competitors are unable to keep up according to

the company, leading to incremental share gains. In terms of its 3 largest AI
customers’ CCL supply chain, the company continues to target 50%+ market share in
the long term.
(2) The company expects target capacity to reach 9.30mn sheets/month by 2027
end (slightly below the previous 9.45mn sheets/month target due to allocation of
space for office construction). Despite the minor adjustment, management believes
the company is well positioned to capture additional market share through its

proactive expansion plan, while Japanese and Korean peers continue to expand at a
much slower pace.
(3) The company is entering the high-end AI substrate CCL industry with the new
300k sheets/month capacity in Taiwan released in 4Q26 dedicated to this new
business (the company’s AI substrate CCL capacity could reach ~1mn sheets/month
if fully converted). The company is entering this market to capture market share
amid supply shortage, with a focus on serving customers in Taiwan and mainland

China.
(4) The MP (mass production) for M9 CCL will start from 2H26, and management
expects Nvidia to be the first to adopt M9 CCL, with ASIC customers to follow suit.
The highest end CCL that most ASIC projects currently utilize is M8+ grade, with a
high probability of migrating to M9 grade in 2027. EMC believes the M9 CCL will
contribute at least low single digits % of total revenue in 2026 and could move up to
double digits % of total revenue in 2027.

(5) The company continues to make progress with M8.5/M9 CCL, which is
differentiated by glass fiber type (low-DK for M8.5 and Q-glass for M9), and
indicated that both solutions remain viable at this stage, while final adoption is still
dependent on Nvidia, and backplane is still one year away from production, thus
design is not finalized yet.
(6) For high-end product (M7 and above grade CCL) revenue contribution, the
company believes the number should go up from 50%+ in 2025 to at least 60% in

### Page 2

Investment view
We maintain our positive view on EMC considering the market share gain opportunities
in ASIC driven by a major CSP client doubling the demand in 2027, while a key Japanese
competitor has no capacity expansion plan and is unable to meet the incremental
demand. The company’s proactive capacity expansion plans (26% 2025-27E CAGR
capacity expansion vs. industry average at 15-20% 2025-27E CAGR) should continue to
lead to high market share in the high-end CCL industry. Moreover, considering the

company’s leadership position in technology, we expect the company should be the
major supplier for M9 grade CCL in the next 12-24months, with ASP for M9 grade CCL at
over 5x vs. EMC’s current product average ASP , suggesting solid revenue upside potential
for EMC in coming quarters/years.
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for

3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+%
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over

time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a

solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive.
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2)
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$6,000 is based on 27x 2027E P/E (in line
with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising

trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3)
rising competition from mainland China peers.

GS Factor Profile
The Goldman Sachs Factor Profile provides investment context for a stock by comparing key attributes to the market (i.e. our universe of rated stocks)
and its sector peers. The four key attributes depicted are: Growth, Financial Returns, Multiple (e.g. valuation) and Integrated (a c omposite of Growth,
Financial Returns and Multiple). Growth, Financial Returns and Multiple are calculated by using normalized ranks for specific metrics f or each stock. The

normalized ranks for the metrics are then averaged and converted into percentiles for the relevant attribute. The precise calcula tion of each metric may
vary depending on the fiscal y ear, industry and region, but the standard approach is as follows:
Growth is based on a stock’s forward-looking sales growth, EBITDA growth and EPS growth (for financial s tocks, only EPS and sales growth), with a
higher percentile indicating a higher growth company. Financial Returns is based on a stock’s forward-looking ROE, ROCE and CROCI (for financial

stocks, only ROE), with a higher percentile indicating a company with higher financial r eturns. Multiple is based on a stock’s forward-looking P/E, P/B,
price/dividend (P/D), EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF and EV/Debt Adjusted Cash Flow (DACF) (for financial s tocks, only P/E, P/B and P/D), with a higher percentile
indicating a stock trading at a higher multiple. The Integrated percentile is calculated as the average of the Growth percentile, Financial Returns

percentile and (100% - Multiple percentile).
Financial Returns and Multiple use the Goldman Sachs analyst forecasts at the fiscal y
ear-end at least three quarters in the future. Growth uses inputs
for the fiscal y ear at least seven quarters in the future compared with the year at least three quarters in the future (on a per-share basis for all metrics).
For a more detailed description of how we calculate the GS Factor Profile, please c ontact your GS representative.

M&A Rank
Across our global coverage, we examine stocks using an M&A framework, considering both qualitative factors and quantitative factors (which may vary
across sectors and regions) to incorporate the potential that certain companies could be acquired. We then assign a M&A rank as a means of sc oring
companies under our rated coverage from 1 to 3, with 1 representing high (30%-50%) probability of the company becoming an acquisition targ et, 2
representing medium (15%-30%) probability and 3 representing low (0%-15%) probability. For companies ranked 1 or 2, in line with our s tandard

departmental guidelines we incorporate an M&A component into our target price. M&A rank of 3 is considered immaterial and theref ore does not
factor into our price target, and may or may not be discussed in research.
Quantum
Quantum is Goldman Sachs’ proprietary database providing access to detailed financial statement histories, forecasts and ratios. It can be used for
in-depth analysis of a single company, or to make comparisons between companies in differ ent sectors and markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a B uy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not C overed), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region ’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the r eturn across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment R eview Committee or

other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such s tocks.
Total return potential represents the upside or downside differ ential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all cov ered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership .

Greater China Technology Semiconductors

Industry View Attractive
Old Memory: Readacross from
Kioxia IR Day
Legacy NAND technology powers high performance SSD: On its IR Day (link),

Kioxia (covered by Kazuo Yoshikawa) highlighted its GP series high-performance
SSD, which features 100m+ IOPS, ultra-fast and low-latency performance to
accelerate next-gen AI. The company said it is compatible with Nvidia’s Storage-Next
solution. The GP series SSD is used to expand GPU memory to resolve system
memory constraints, and enables large-scale DB
(database) and faster generation.
The GP series SSD (XL-Flash) has adopted Kioxia's proprietary SLC chip.

Implications for Greater China memory stocks: We mentioned in our note, Old
Memory: Upside Surprise Ahead, that legacy NAND technology could be ideal for
fast reading and writing speeds and hence could see expanded TAM from adoption
in datacenters. We view the confirmation from Kioxia as positive for GigaDevice,
Winbond, and Macronix, which are legacy NAND vendors in our coverage and remain
OW on all three stocks.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Macronix International Co Ltd (2337.TW)
Base case, P/B, in line with our Greater China memory coverage, due to the memory industry's
cyclical nature. We apply a target multiple of 7.0x to our 2026 BVPS estimate, vs. its historical
average of 1.5x since 2017. We continue to believe the P/B target is justifiable, as The MLC and
legacy TLC NAND could be in greater shortage into 2H26, with under supply easily going up

to 40%. The only supplier which could fill the gap remains Macronix.
Risks to Upside
n Wider legacy NAND supply / demand gap
n NOR up-cycle, driven by limited supply expansion and/or stronger demand
n Faster growth for Switch hardware and core titles
Risks to Downside
n Less severe legacy NAND supply / demand gap
n NOR down-cycle, driven by faster supply expansion and/or demand weakness
n Slower growth for Switch hardware and core titles
Winbond Electronics Corp (2344.TW)

Base case, 5.5x 2026e P/B. This method is in line with the approach we adopt for Greater China
memory IDM peers, in view of the industry's high volatility. We believe the stock merits a mul-
tiple beyond the high end of its historical band of 0.5-1.5x since 2017, as we are seeing DRAM
pricing upside, a sustainable NOR price hike, and opportunities in SLC NAND and SiCap
foundry business.
Risks to Upside
n NOR Flash pricing increase, driven by stronger-than-expected demand

n Stronger-than-expected DRAM pricing given ongoing supply shortage
n Faster-than-expected SLC NAND development
Risks to Downside
n NOR Flash downcycle, driven by weaker demand
n Lower-than-expected DRAM pricing given ongoing oversupply
n Slower-than-expected SLC NAND development
GigaDevice Semiconductor Beijing Inc (603986.SS)
Our price target is our base case value, derived from our residual income model. Key assump-
tions: cost of equity of 8.9% (beta 1.1, risk-free rate 2% and risk premium 6%), a payout ratio

of 40%, medium-term growth rate of 16.4%, and a terminal growth rate of 3.0%.
Risks to Upside
n NOR up-cycle driven by stronger demand
n Superior chip design, leading to increasing exposure to low-density NOR
n Faster-than-expected DRAM development

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

## Document 103: 260604_daiwa_Taiwan Datacentre Hardware

Source PDF: all report pdf/260604_daiwa_Taiwan Datacentre Hardware.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-06-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: During Computex 2026, we arranged booth tours for our investors with 
representatives of companies within our coverage, including Wiwynn, Aspeed, Delta, Hon 
Hai, and Chenbro, alongside several other companies. In conjunction with keynote 
addresses delivered by leading global technology companies, we believe the primary focus 
of Computex 2026 remained centered on the CPU domain - specifically, the role of CPUs 
in orchestrating the broader ecosystem for agentic AI, as well as the emerging total 
addressable market (TAM) arising from AI PCs incorporating NVIDIA's Spark chipset. 
Across the booths we visited, the majority of vendors showcased components and 
solutions applicable to NVIDIA's Vera Rubin generation platform. These demonstrations 
should be regarded as indicative of trial production activity within the Taiwan supply chain 
ahead of the official volume ramp, which is anticipated to commence in 4Q26, with the 
underlying concepts having been primarily introduced at GTC. With respect to key 
technology transitions, the developments that garnered the most attention at the 
conference included CPO-based AI server architecture, Co-Packaging Connectors (CPC), 
High-Voltage Direct Current (HVDC) power delivery, and microgrid solutions. In conclusion, 
we encourage investors to maintain a sustained focus on CPU-related investment themes, 
as well as on specification upgrades driven primarily by escalating high-speed 
transmission requirements. Our key Buy ideas include: Aspeed, Lotes (3533 TT, 
TWD2,400), Wiwynn, Delta, TUC (6274 TT, TWD1,695), EMC (2383 TT, TWD4,830), and 
Accton (2345 TT, TWD2,510).

Wiwynn (6669 TT, TWD5,570, Buy [1]) 
• According to investor feedback, Wiwynn's booth was widely regarded as one of the 
most compelling exhibitions at Computex, owing to its demonstration of a Co-Packaged 
Optics (CPO) computing rack. The demonstration was developed in collaboration with 
several key partners, including Ayar Labs - contributing the optical engine and External 
Laser Small Form Factor Pluggable (ELSFP) technology - and GUC (3443 TT 
TWD4,400, Hold [3], covered by Rick Hsu), contributing the XPU. It is our understanding 
that the demonstration remains under development, and the collaboration with Ayar 
Labs is currently in a pilot stage. Wiwynn's objective is to ensure that it acquires the 
requisite capabilities and expertise ahead of CPO technology reaching broader 
commercial maturity. Based on our observations, Wiwynn's focus appears to be 
concentrated on computing tray development, while the scale-out and scale-up switch 
racks are expected to be contributed by other partners. 
• Another notable highlight from Wiwynn's exhibition was its demonstration of AMD's 
Helios platform. Based on our understanding, AMD's Helios architecture would comprise 
18 computing units, each configured with four GPUs and one CPU per rack. Given the 
physical dimensions of AMD's MI450 series GPU and connectivity speed requirements, 
the GPU layout is positioned at the bottom of the PCB in order to optimize connectivity 
with the scale-up system. According to Wiwynn, the Helios platform will leverage UALink 
for scale-up interconnects, while adopting Ethernet for scale-out connectivity 
(implementing UALink over Ethernet).

Aspeed (5274 TT, TWD18,265, Buy [1]) 
• At Computex 2026, Aspeed's showcase highlighted its evolution from a pure Baseboard 
Management Controller (BMC) supplier toward a broader platform management and

 Our key takeaways from 2026 Computex

infrastructure control provider. The company demonstrated solutions spanning BMC, 
Satellite Management Controller (SMC, or mini-BMC), I/O expansion, and security IC. 
• A key emphasis at Aspeed's booth was the company's adoption of RISC-V architecture 
and OCP open-source frameworks to support Caliptra — an IP designed to execute 
root-of-trust functions. This security enhancement is expected to be implemented across 
the majority of Aspeed's product lines, thereby elevating the overall security standard of 
its devices. 
• Another significant highlight was the introduction of the AST1840, succeeding the 
previous AST1800. The AST1700/1800/1840 series constitutes Aspeed's I/O expander 
product line, with the AST1800 marking the first edition to incorporate an embedded 
FPGA within the I/O expander. With the AST1840, Aspeed has transitioned to 
outsourcing the FPGA chip from Lattice Semiconductor, resulting in the development of 
a new System-on-Chip (SoC) for the AST1840. 
• Regarding the near-term outlook, following the company's revenue guidance of 
TWD4.1-4.3 billion, we anticipate an additional revenue step-up from 4Q26, as Aspeed 
has qualified with a second OSAT partner, enabling broader support from substrate 
vendors. With respect to the T-glass to E-glass substrate certification, we expect the 
transition to be completed successfully; however, the initial production batch of the 
AST2700 will incorporate substrates containing both T-glass and E-glass. Over the 
longer term, only the E-glass substrate version is expected to remain in production. 
• The company expressed a continued positive outlook on CPU server demand, driven by 
the growth of agentic AI workloads. According to Aspeed's latest forecast, BMC unit 
shipments are projected to reach approximately 8.5 million units in 2026. Looking ahead 
to 2027, we think BMC shipments from the conventional server segment will grow by at 
least 30% year-over-year.

Delta Electronics (2308 TT, TWD2,425, Buy [1]) 
• Delta’s Computex 2026 showcase its strategy of extending from power and thermal 
components into broader AI datacentre infrastructure. Delta highlighted five main 
exhibition areas: modular datacentre, micro grid solutions, HVDC solutions, Vera Rubin 
solutions, and Edge AI. We believe the key investment implication is that Delta is 
positioning itself not only as a supplier of power supplies and cooling components, but 
also as an infrastructure partner spanning power conversion. 
• 800VDC remains the core product direction. Delta’s key AI datacentre message is built 
around 800VDC architecture. Its showcase included an 800VDC in-row power system, 
90kW DC-DC power shelf, 110kW AC-DC power shelf, 12kW power distribution board, 
and DC-DC conversion modules. The 800VDC in-row power system is designed to 
simplify the power conversion architecture and achieve over 98% system efficiency, 
while also integrating 480kW of total BBU for short-duration support to critical loads. We 
see this as the next step in Delta’s power roadmap as AI rack power emerge. 
• Micro-grid is becoming the next key question from investors on top of HVDC, while SST 
and SOFC becomes the key discussion. However, these new growth drivers likely won’t 
bear fruit in just 1-2 years. The microgrid solution integrates renewables, energy 
storage, fuel cells and other power sources to support datacentre operation in grid-
connected and off-grid modes. Delta’s solid-state transformer (SST) can directly convert 
medium-voltage AC to low-voltage 800VDC, with high power density and modular 
design to reduce footprint and power losses. The SOFC fuel cell offers 60% generation 
efficiency and 24-hour stable power supply with modular expansion. We see SST and 
SOFC as strategic long-term options tied to AI datacentre power trend, while the nearer-
term investment focus remains 800VDC power racks, power shelves, distribution 
boards, BBU, and liquid cooling.

### Page 3

Taiwan Datacentre Hardware 
4 June 2026 
3

Hon Hai (2317 TT, TWD293.0, Buy [1]) 
• As one of NVIDIA's key strategic partners, Hon Hai presented its comprehensive lineup 
of NVIDIA AI servers at Computex. Notably, the Vera CPU rack and CPO switch were 
absent from the exhibition. Hon Hai's demonstration included new computing trays and 
switches for the VR200, as well as the HGX NVL8 — a new HGX configuration featuring 
eight GPUs, dual x86 CPUs, and integrated liquid-cooling components. The compact 
design of the NVL8 enables a reduced form factor of 2U (with a 1U height of 45mm). 
• In addition, Hon Hai showcased its Co-Packaging Connector (CPC), designed for 
integration into switches such as the Spectrum-X. Based on our understanding, Hon 
Hai/FIT is capable of delivering 224 Gbps per lane, achieving an aggregate throughput 
of 12.8 Tbps per connector. We believe that at least two clients are currently evaluating 
the adoption of this CPC technology.

• From FIT's perspective, the company also presented its Paladin connector, intended for 
use within the mid-plane of the computing tray. This connector, along with the mid-plane, 
is expected to serve as a key replacement for Amphenol's flyover cable. We believe FIT 
is actively developing a next-generation connector solution to be deployed within the 
Kyber architecture, where the mid-plane will serve as the interconnect between both the 
computing and switch trays.

Chenbro (8210 TT, TWD1,420, Buy [1]) 
• As an established rack vendor on NVIDIA's Reference Vendor List (RVL), Chenbro 
showcased several rack solutions compatible with NVIDIA's VR NVL72 at the exhibition. 
• We understand that Chenbro is currently engaged in multiple new rack projects, 
encompassing noise-cancellation chassis, IT racks for NVIDIA/AMD, liquid-cooling racks 
(including CDU racks in both Liquid-to-Liquid and Liquid-to-Air configurations), and 
switch racks. This strategic expansion underscores Chenbro's transition from a chassis 
vendor to a full-scale rack vendor, reflecting the broader industry shift whereby end 
customers are increasingly designing their infrastructure at the rack level rather than the 
chassis level. 
• We anticipate that the margin profile for rack solutions will reach c.25-30%, compared to 
our previous estimate of c.15-20%, driven by the significant degree of customization 
involved. Consequently, our revised long-term gross margin forecast has been lifted 
from 25-26% to 28-29%.  
• During the booth tour, Chenbro emphasized its enhanced development cycle, which is 
designed to further reduce time-to-market for its clients. The company also highlighted 
its diversified development teams - comprising software engineers, industrial designers, 
mechanical engineers, and other specialists - to provide comprehensive support to end 
customers. We believe Chenbro's role is a key co-design and the most fast partner for 
its end customers, making it a critical differentiator from its competitors and mitigating 
the impact of competitive pressures.

Pegatron (4938.TW): Computex 2026: AI server in expansion; AI
infrastructure product portfolio showcased
4 June 2026 | 9:13PM HKT
We visited Pegatron’s booth on June 2 at our Computex tour in Taipei. Pegatron
showcased its AI infrastructure across cloud and edge AI, as well as quadruped
robot. Management remains positive on their AI server business growth and expects
continuous growth in coming years.
Key takeaways: Pegatron showcased its complete next-generation AI infrastructure

product portfolio at Computex (link), such as Nvidia Vera Rubin NVL72, Nvidia HGX
Rubin NVL8, and AMD GPU servers, focusing on the deployment of AI Factory,
High-Performance Computing (HPC), and AI data center. Pegatron’s 400G, 800G, and
1.6T switch were also showcased to fulfill the high-speed and lo w latency data
transmission requirements of AI data center. Management sees the growth
opportunities in AI infrastructure and expects a continuous expansion for its AI

server business in coming years, supported by the company’s transformation and
expansion from consumer electronics to AI servers. Besides AI infrastructures,
Pegatron also launched its next generation SIMBA II quadruped robot platform,
capturing the growth opportunities in edge AI computing and supporting the
company’s revenue growth ahead.

GS Factor Profile
The Goldman Sachs Factor Profile provides investment context for a stock by comparing key attributes to the market (i.e. our universe of rated stocks)
and its sector peers. The four key attributes depicted are: Growth, Financial Returns, Multiple (e.g. valuation) and Integrated (a c omposite of Growth,
Financial Returns and Multiple). Growth, Financial Returns and Multiple are calculated by using normalized ranks for specific metrics f or each stock. The

normalized ranks for the metrics are then averaged and converted into percentiles for the relevant attribute. The precise calcula tion of each metric may
vary depending on the fiscal y ear, industry and region, but the standard approach is as follows:
Growth is based on a stock’s forward-looking sales growth, EBITDA growth and EPS growth (for financial s tocks, only EPS and sales growth), with a
higher percentile indicating a higher growth company. Financial Returns is based on a stock’s forward-looking ROE, ROCE and CROCI (for financial

stocks, only ROE), with a higher percentile indicating a company with higher financial r eturns. Multiple is based on a stock’s forward-looking P/E, P/B,
price/dividend (P/D), EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF and EV/Debt Adjusted Cash Flow (DACF) (for financial s tocks, only P/E, P/B and P/D), with a higher percentile
indicating a stock trading at a higher multiple. The Integrated percentile is calculated as the average of the Growth percentile, Financial Returns

percentile and (100% - Multiple percentile).
Financial Returns and Multiple use the Goldman Sachs analyst forecasts at the fiscal y ear-end at least three quarters in the future. Growth uses inputs
for the fiscal y ear at least seven quarters in the future compared with the year at least three quarters in the future (on a per-share basis for all metrics).
For a more detailed description of how we calculate the GS Factor Profile, please c ontact your GS representative.

M&A Rank
Across our global coverage, we examine stocks using an M&A framework, considering both qualitative factors and quantitative factors (which may vary
across sectors and regions) to incorporate the potential that certain companies could be acquired. We then assign a M&A rank as a means of sc oring
companies under our rated coverage from 1 to 3, with 1 representing high (30%-50%) probability of the company becoming an acquisition targ et, 2
representing medium (15%-30%) probability and 3 representing low (0%-15%) probability. For companies ranked 1 or 2, in line with our s tandard

departmental guidelines we incorporate an M&A component into our target price. M&A rank of 3 is considered immaterial and theref ore does not
factor into our price target, and may or may not be discussed in research.
Quantum
Quantum is Goldman Sachs’ proprietary database providing access to detailed financial statement histories, forecasts and ratios. It can be used for
in-depth analysis of a single company, or to make comparisons between companies in differ ent sectors and markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a B uy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not C overed), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region ’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the r eturn across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment R eview Committee or

other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such s tocks.
Total return potential represents the upside or downside differ ential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all cov ered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership .

### Page 9

Earnings revision and where we stand vs. consensus
Earnings revisions
We revise up our 2026/27/28E EPS by 1/5/24% to factor in higher ASP per watt for its AI
PSU business and higher penetration for its power rack business (we raise up revenue
estimates by 2/23% for 2027/28E). For the HVDC solution, we expect demand in late
2026 with faster progress from the +-400VDC solutions, and continue to expect
stronger HVDC demand in 2027E/2028E. Therefore, we revise up our 2026/27/28E GM

by 0.1/0.5/0.1ppt due to the slight margin expansion driven by better scale.
Where are we vs. consensus?
Our 2026/27/28E revenue estimates are 7/20/47% higher than Bloomberg consensus,
due to our positive view on the AI power related business opportunity (we believe AI
power revenue, including AC/DC, DC/DC and power rack products, will reach
NT$156bn/NT$486bn/NT$1,434bn in 2026/27/28E). As such, considering our high
